=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a tree with N \times M + 1 vertices numbered 0, 1, \dots, N \times M. The i-th edge (1 \leq i \leq N \times M) connects vertices i and \max(i - N, 0).
Vertex 0 is painted. The other vertices are unpainted.
Takahashi is at vertex 0. As long as there exists an unpainted vertex, he performs the following operation:

- He chooses one of the vertices adjacent to his current vertex uniformly at random (all choices are independent) and moves to that vertex. Then, if the vertex he is on is unpainted, he paints it.

Find the expected number of times he performs the operation, modulo 998244353.

What is the expected value modulo 998244353?

It can be proved that the sought expected value is always rational. Under the constraints of this problem, when that value is expressed as an irreducible fraction \frac{P}{Q}, it can also be proved that Q \not\equiv 0 \pmod{998244353}. Then, there uniquely exists an integer R such that R \times Q \equiv P \pmod{998244353}, 0 \leq R \lt 998244353. Report this R.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M

Output

Print the expected number of times he performs the operation, modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^5
- N and M are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 2

Sample Output 1

20

For example, Takahashi could behave as follows.

- Moves to vertex 1 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 0. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 1. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 3 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 1. This action is chosen with probability 1.
- Moves to vertex 0. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 2 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 4 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.

He behaves in this way with probability \frac{1}{128}, in which case the number of operations is 8. The expected number of operations is 20.

Sample Input 2

123456 185185

Sample Output 2

69292914

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Edge cases:
- N=1, M large: H_N=1, answer = M^2*(2*1*1 -1)=M^2. Good.

Let's derive modular inverse precompute:
MOD = 998244353
inv = [0]*(N+1)
inv[1]=1
for i in range(2,N+1):
    inv[i] = MOD - (MOD//i) * inv[MOD%i] % MOD
H=0
for i in range(1,N+1):
    H = (H + inv[i]) % MOD
ans = (M % MOD) * (M % MOD) % MOD
factor = (2 * (N % MOD) % MOD * H % MOD - 1) % MOD
ans = ans * factor % MOD
print(ans)

Now, let's revisit the proof of E[K] = N M H_N. We used q=1/M. Let's derive E[K] more rigorously:
Excursions are i.i.d. Each excursion:
- Choose arm A uniform among N.
- Success S=1 if it reaches leaf before returning to root; P(S=1)=1/M independent of A.
Thus successful excursions form a sequence of i.i.d. uniform arms, and the number of trials between successes is geometric with mean M. Let K_s = number of successful excursions needed to collect all N arms. E[K_s]=N H_N. The total number of excursions K is the trial number of the K_s-th success. Since success indicators independent of arms, E[K | K_s] = M K_s? Wait if success prob q=1/M, expected trials for k successes is k/q = k M. So E[K] = M E[K_s] = N M H_N. Yes.

But note: The number of unsuccessful excursions before the first success has mean (1-q)/q = M-1. After each success, if not all arms collected, need continue. The total expected unsuccessful excursions = (M-1) E[K_s]? Because between successes, expected failures M-1, and after final success no failures. So E[K] = E[K_s] + (M-1)E[K_s] = M E[K_s]. Good.

Now, E[L] = 2M. Let's verify with q and expected duration of successful/unsuccessful maybe. For M=2, E[L]=4. import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0]); M = int(data[1])
    inv = [0]*(N+1)
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N+1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
    h = 0
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD? But h + inv[i] can exceed 2*MOD? inv[i] < MOD, h < MOD, sum <2MOD, so one subtract enough. Use h = (h + inv[i]) % MOD for clarity.
    factor = (2 * (N % MOD) % MOD * h - 1) % MOD
    ans = (M % MOD) * (M % MOD) % MOD
    ans = ans * factor % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

W_A = 1 + 0.5 F1 + 0.5 V_a.
U_A = 1 + 0.5 F0 + 0.5 V_a.
F0 = 1 + U_A (since both arms symmetric, choose A; after move to A, expected U_A). Actually F0 = 1 + U_A.

Let's solve. Variables F0,F1,U_A,U1,V_a,W_A.

Equations:
(1) F0 = 1 + U_A.
(2) U_A = 1 + 0.5 F0 + 0.5 V_a.
(3) V_a = 1 + W_A.
(4) W_A = 1 + 0.5 F1 + 0.5 V_a.
(5) F1 = 1 + 0.5 W_A + 0.5 U1.
(6) U1 = 1 + 0.5 F1.

Solve:
From (6): U1 = 1 + 0.5 F1.
(5): F1 = 1 + 0.5 W_A + 0.5(1 + 0.5 F1) = 1 + 0.5 W_A + 0.5 + 0.25 F1 = 1.5 + 0.5 W_A + 0.25 F1 => 0.75 F1 = 1.5 + 0.5 W_A => F1 = 2 + (2/3) W_A.

(3): V_a = 1 + W_A.
(4): W_A = 1 + 0.5 F1 + 0.5(1+W_A) = 1 + 0.5 F1 + 0.5 + 0.5 W_A = 1.5 + 0.5 F1 + 0.5 W_A => 0.5 W_A = 1.5 + 0.5 F1 => W_A = 3 + F1.
Combine with F1 = 2 + (2/3)W_A => F1 = 2 + (2/3)(3+F1)=2+2+(2/3)F1=4+(2/3)F1 => (1/3)F1=4 => F1=12. Then W_A=15, V_a=16.

Now (2): U_A = 1 + 0.5 F0 + 0.5*16 = 1 + 0.5 F0 + 8 = 9 + 0.5 F0.
(1): F0 = 1 + U_A = 10 + 0.5 F0 => 0.5 F0=10 => F0=20. Matches sample. Great. Our formula is correct.

Wald's equation holds for i.i.d. X_t and stopping time K with finite expectation. But let's prove quickly: E[∑_{t=1}^K X_t] = ∑_{t≥1} E[X_t 1_{K≥t}]. Since {K≥t} is determined by X_1,...,X_{t-1} (and maybe X_t? For stopping time, {K≥t} is complement of {K<t}, and {K<t} depends on X_1,...,X_{t-1}; so {K≥t} is independent of X_t? Actually {K<t} = ∪_{s<t} {K=s}, each depends on X_1,...,X_s, so yes depends only on X_1,...,X_{t-1}. Thus {K≥t} is independent of X_t. Therefore E[X_t 1_{K≥t}] = E[X_t] P(K≥t). Sum = E[X_1] ∑ P(K≥t) = E[X_1] E[K]. This is a standard proof. It requires X_t i.i.d. and K stopping time w.r.t. X_t. Our K is determined by S_t and A_t, which are part of X_t=(A_t,S_t,L_t). But wait, K is defined as number of excursions until all arms have at least one success. If all arms already have success after some excursion, K is that excursion index. It is a stopping time. Good.

Now, E[K] = N M H_N. Let's verify via Wald maybe with success indicators? We can compute E[K] directly:
Let K_s = number of successes in first K excursions. K_s is a stopping time w.r.t. the subsequence of successful arms? Actually K_s = number of successful excursions until all arms seen. The success indicators and arms are i.i.d. We can model each excursion as a "trial" with success prob q=1/M and a coupon type if success. The number of trials K until coupon collection complete. This is a Markov chain; E[K] can be derived by states: Let E_i be expected remaining excursions when i distinct arms have been seen. From state i, each excursion:
- success on new arm: prob ((N-i)/N) * q
- success on old arm: prob (i/N)*q
- failure: prob 1-q
Then E_i = 1 + q*(i/N) E_i + q*((N-i)/N) E_{i+1} + (1-q) E_i? Wait after failure, state same. Solve:
E_i = 1 + (1-q)E_i + q*(i/N)E_i + q*((N-i)/N)E_{i+1}
=> q*((N-i)/N) E_i = 1 + q*((N-i)/N) E_{i+1}
=> E_i = N/(q(N-i)) + E_{i+1}.
Sum i=0..N-1: E_0 = N/q * H_N = N M H_N. Good. This is an alternative derivation. Nice.

Let's write final code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    N, M = map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split())
    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    inv[1] = 1
    h = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD
    factor = (2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD
    factor = (factor - 1) % MOD
    ans = (M % MOD) * (M % MOD) % MOD
    ans = ans * factor % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "excursion success probability q=1/M" for M=1: starting at depth1 which is leaf, probability hit leaf before root =1. For M=2: starting at depth1, probability hit depth2 before root =1/2. For general M: gambler's ruin with absorbing boundaries 0 and M, starting at1, probability hit M before0 =1/M. Yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "expected return time to root =2M" via stationary distribution. Let's verify with M=1,N=2: expected return time to root =2M=2. Starting at root, choose leaf, then return root: 2 operations. Yes. For M=2,N=2: expected return time to root =4. Starting at root, choose arm, expected time to return root =4. We computed. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "full duration L_t" and "E[K]" maybe if K is number of excursions until all arms have at least one success, then E[K] = NM H_N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

K_s coupon collector successes =3 expected. Among K excursions, expected unsuccessful =3, successful=3. Unsuccessful full duration mean? For M=2, unsuccessful duration =2 always? Let's check unsuccessful excursion: root->1, then from1 must go root (since if go leaf it would be successful). So unsuccessful duration=2. Successful full duration mean? E[L|success] = (E[L] - (1-q)*2)/q = (4 -0.5*2)/0.5 =6. So E∑ L = E[#unsuccess]*2 + E[#success]*6 =3*2+3*6=24. Good. E[K]E[L]=6*4=24. Works.

= E[K_s]*E[L|success] - M^2 + E[unsuccess]*E[L|fail].
Since E[K]E[L] = E[K_s]E[L|success] + E[unsuccess]E[L|fail], same. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "E[K] = N M H_N" maybe if q=1/M, then expected number of excursions to get one success for a specific arm? For a specific arm, the number of excursions until a successful excursion into that arm is geometric with success prob q/N =1/(NM), mean NM. For N arms, coupon collector with each coupon rate q/N? The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.

Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "subtract M^2" if the final excursion's return time after leaf hit is not exactly M^2 but maybe the expected time from leaf to root in the original graph with root degree N? Starting at leaf M, the walk moves along the arm; when it reaches root, the excursion would end. The root degree doesn't affect the path before reaching root because at root the excursion would end; at depth1, neighbors root and depth2. The expected hitting time to root starting at leaf M is the same e_M = M^2, independent of N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "time to leaf hit in final excursion" maybe if the final leaf is hit not as the first time that arm's leaf is hit? It must be the first time for that arm because if it had been hit before, that arm's leaf would already be painted. So final excursion is the first successful excursion for that arm. But our K process stops when all arms have had at least one success; the final successful excursion is the first success for the last arm. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "successful excursions" for an arm after it has already been painted: They can occur, but K stops at the first time all arms have had at least one success, so no excursions after K. Good.

The expected time to collect all N coupons in continuous time is N M H_N? Let's check: Each excursion is a trial; probability of a new coupon when i coupons collected = (N-i)/N * q = (N-i)/(NM). Expected trials to get next new coupon = NM/(N-i). Sum i=0..N-1 = NM H_N. Yes. This is a nice direct derivation. Good.
